=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is a 50 x 50 chessboard with one knight and some pawns on it. You are given two integers kx and ky where (kx, ky) denotes the position of the knight, and a 2D array positions where positions[i] = [x_i, y_i] denotes the position of the pawns on the chessboard.
Alice and Bob play a turn-based game, where Alice goes first. In each player's turn:

The player selects a pawn that still exists on the board and captures it with the knight in the fewest possible moves. Note that the player can select any pawn, it might not be one that can be captured in the least number of moves.
In the process of capturing the selected pawn, the knight may pass other pawns without capturing them. Only the selected pawn can be captured in this turn.

Alice is trying to maximize the sum of the number of moves made by both players until there are no more pawns on the board, whereas Bob tries to minimize them.
Return the maximum total number of moves made during the game that Alice can achieve, assuming both players play optimally.
Note that in one move, a chess knight has eight possible positions it can move to, as illustrated below. Each move is two cells in a cardinal direction, then one cell in an orthogonal direction.

 
Example 1:

Input: kx = 1, ky = 1, positions = [[0,0]]
Output: 4
Explanation:

The knight takes 4 moves to reach the pawn at (0, 0).

Example 2:

Input: kx = 0, ky = 2, positions = [[1,1],[2,2],[3,3]]
Output: 8
Explanation:


Alice picks the pawn at (2, 2) and captures it in two moves: (0, 2) -> (1, 4) -> (2, 2).
Bob picks the pawn at (3, 3) and captures it in two moves: (2, 2) -> (4, 1) -> (3, 3).
Alice picks the pawn at (1, 1) and captures it in four moves: (3, 3) -> (4, 1) -> (2, 2) -> (0, 3) -> (1, 1).


Example 3:

Input: kx = 0, ky = 0, positions = [[1,2],[2,4]]
Output: 3
Explanation:

Alice picks the pawn at (2, 4) and captures it in two moves: (0, 0) -> (1, 2) -> (2, 4). Note that the pawn at (1, 2) is not captured.
Bob picks the pawn at (1, 2) and captures it in one move: (2, 4) -> (1, 2).


 
Constraints:

0 <= kx, ky <= 49
1 <= positions.length <= 15
positions[i].length == 2
0 <= positions[i][0], positions[i][1] <= 49
All positions[i] are unique.
The input is generated such that positions[i] != [kx, ky] for all 0 <= i < positions.length.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Option 1:
class Solution:
    def maxMoves(self, kx: int, ky: int, positions: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        n = len(positions)
        # BFS function
        from collections import deque
        def bfs(sx,sy):
            dist = [[-1]*50 for _ in range(50)]
            dist[sx][sy]=0
            q=deque([(sx,sy)])
            while q:
                x,y=q.popleft()
                for dx,dy in moves:
                    nx,ny=x+dx,y+dy
                    if 0<=nx<50 and 0<=ny<50 and dist[nx][ny]==-1:
                        dist[nx][ny]=dist[x][y]+1
                        q.append((nx,ny))
            return dist
        start_dist = bfs(kx,ky)
        pawn_dist = [bfs(x,y) for x,y in positions]
        # dist[i][j]
        dist = [[0]*n for _ in range(n)]
        for i in range(n):
            g = pawn_dist[i]
            for j in range(n):
                dist[i][j] = g[positions[j][0]][positions[j][1]]
        start = [start_dist[x][y] for x,y in positions]
        full = (1<<n)-1
        from functools import lru_cache
        @lru_cache(None)
        def dp(mask, cur):
            if mask == 0:
                return 0
            # Determine turn: number of pawns remaining? Actually removed = n - mask.bit_count()
            # Alice if removed even. But if cur == n? initial? We'll handle separately.
            # For dp called with cur in 0..n-1.
            if ((n - mask.bit_count()) & 1) == 0:
                best = -1
                for j in range(n):
                    if mask & (1<<j):
                        val = dist[cur][j] + dp(mask ^ (1<<j), j)
                        if val > best: best = val
                return best
            else:
                best = 10**9
                for j in range(n):
                    if mask & (1<<j):
                        val = dist[cur][j] + dp(mask ^ (1<<j), j)
                        if val < best: best = val
                return best
        # Initial turn Alice removed=0 even
        ans = -1
        for j in range(n):
            val = start[j] + dp(full ^ (1<<j), j)
            if val > ans: ans = val
        return ans

Need check parity in dp: n - mask.bit_count() = number of pawns already captured. For state with mask remaining and current cur (last captured), yes. But what about dp called with mask=0 and cur? base. For initial, removed=0 even, Alice. Good.

        else:
            best = INF
            ...

Now, let's think about code style:
```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def maxMoves(self, kx: int, ky: int, positions: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        n = len(positions)
        moves = [(1,2),(1,-2),(-1,2),(-1,-2),(2,1),(2,-1),(-2,1),(-2,-1)]
        def bfs(sx, sy):
            dist = [[-1]*50 for _ in range(50)]
            dist[sx][sy] = 0
            q = deque([(sx, sy)])
            while q:
                x, y = q.popleft()
                for dx, dy in moves:
                    nx, ny = x+dx, y+dy
                    if 0 <= nx < 50 and 0 <= ny < 50 and dist[nx][ny] == -1:
                        dist[nx][ny] = dist[x][y] + 1
                        q.append((nx, ny))
            return dist

        start_grid = bfs(kx, ky)
        start = [start_grid[x][y] for x, y in positions]

        dist = [[0]*n for _ in range(n)]
        for i, (x, y) in enumerate(positions):
            grid = bfs(x, y)
            for j, (px, py) in enumerate(positions):
                dist[i][j] = grid[px][py]

        full = (1 << n) - 1
        pop = [0] * (1 << n)
        for mask in range(1, 1 << n):
            pop[mask] = pop[mask >> 1] + (mask & 1)

        dp = [[0] * n for _ in range(1 << n)]
        INF = 10**9

        for mask in range(1, 1 << n):
            alice = ((n - pop[mask]) & 1) == 0
            for cur in range(n):
                if mask & (1 << cur):
                    continue
                if alice:
                    best = -1
                    for j in range(n):
                        if mask & (1 << j):
                            val = dist[cur][j] + dp[mask ^ (1 << j)][j]
                            if val > best:
                                best = val
                    dp[mask][cur] = best
                else:
                    best = INF
                    for j in range(n):
                        if mask & (1 << j):
                            val = dist[cur][j] + dp[mask ^ (1 << j)][j]
                            if val < best:
                                best = val
                    dp[mask][cur] = best

        ans = -1
        for j in range(n):
            val = start[j] + dp[full ^ (1 << j)][j]
            if val > ans:
                ans = val
        return ans
```

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def maxMoves(self, kx: int, ky: int, positions: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        n = len(positions)
        size = 50
        moves = [
            (1, 2), (1, -2), (-1, 2), (-1, -2),
            (2, 1), (2, -1), (-2, 1), (-2, -1)
        ]

        def bfs(sx: int, sy: int):
            dist = [[-1] * size for _ in range(size)]
            dist[sx][sy] = 0
            q = deque([(sx, sy)])

            while q:
                x, y = q.popleft()
                for dx, dy in moves:
                    nx, ny = x + dx, y + dy
                    if 0 <= nx < size and 0 <= ny < size and dist[nx][ny] == -1:
                        dist[nx][ny] = dist[x][y] + 1
                        q.append((nx, ny))

            return dist

        start_grid = bfs(kx, ky)
        start_dist = [start_grid[x][y] for x, y in positions]

        dist = [[0] * n for _ in range(n)]
        for i, (x, y) in enumerate(positions):
            grid = bfs(x, y)
            for j, (px, py) in enumerate(positions):
                dist[i][j] = grid[px][py]

        full = (1 << n) - 1

        pop = [0] * (1 << n)
        for mask in range(1, 1 << n):
            pop[mask] = pop[mask >> 1] + (mask & 1)

        dp = [[0] * n for _ in range(1 << n)]
        INF = 10 ** 9

        for mask in range(1, 1 << n):
            alice_turn = ((n - pop[mask]) & 1) == 0

            for cur in range(n):
                if mask & (1 << cur):
                    continue

                if alice_turn:
                    best = -1
                    for j in range(n):
                        if mask & (1 << j):
                            best = max(best, dist[cur][j] + dp[mask ^ (1 << j)][j])
                    dp[mask][cur] = best
                else:
                    best = INF
                    for j in range(n):
                        if mask & (1 << j):
                            best = min(best, dist[cur][j] + dp[mask ^ (1 << j)][j])
                    dp[mask][cur] = best

        ans = -1
        for j in range(n):
            ans = max(ans, start_dist[j] + dp[full ^ (1 << j)][j])

        return ans
```
